import sympy as sp

def solve_demand(n):
    # variables x_1..x_{n-1}
    xs = sp.symbols('x1:'+str(n))
    equations = []
    # x0 = 0, xn = 0
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        left = xs[i-2] if i-2 >=0 else 0  # x_{i-1}
        right = xs[i-1] if i-1 < n-1 else 0  # x_i
        d = 2 if i%2==1 else 0
        equations.append(sp.Eq(left + right, d))
    sol = sp.solve(equations, xs, dict=True)
    return sol

[CODE] (turn 5):
def min_moves_path(n):
    best = None
    best_info = None
    # iterate over start and end positions (1..n)
    for start in range(1, n+1):
        for end in range(start, n+1):  # allow start<=end (order not important)
            # Determine p_i (parity requirement for sum x_{i-1}+x_i)
            p = [0]*(n+1)  # 1-indexed
            # Determine s_i+e_i for each vertex
            s_e = [0]*(n+1)
            if start == end:
                s_e[start] = 2
            else:
                s_e[start] = 1
                s_e[end] = 1
            for i in range(1, n+1):
                p[i] = s_e[i] % 2  # parity requirement
            # Determine minimal L_i (lower bound) for each vertex
            L = [0]*(n+1)
            feasible = True
            for i in range(1, n+1):
                if s_e[i]==0:
                    # interior not start/end
                    if i%2==1:  # odd vertex (T)
                        L[i] = 2
                    else:
                        L[i] = 0
                elif s_e[i]==1:
                    # start or end distinct
                    if i%2==1:  # odd
                        L[i] = 1
                    else:
                        L[i] = 3
                elif s_e[i]==2:
                    # start=end
                    if i%2==1:
                        L[i] = 0
                    else:
                        L[i] = 2
                # Ensure parity matches p
                if (L[i] - p[i]) % 2 != 0:
                    # adjust up to next number with correct parity
                    L[i] += 1
                # L[i] must be >= p[i] (since sum >= p)
                if L[i] < p[i]:
                    L[i] = p[i]
            # Now set up MILP
            # Variables: x[1..n-1], y[1..n]
            c = np.concatenate([np.ones(n-1), np.zeros(n)])  # objective sum x_i
            # constraints: for each i, x_{i-1}+x_i - 2*y_i = p_i
            # We'll set equality constraints using A_eq
            A_eq = []
            b_eq = []
            # Build rows for each i
            for i in range(1, n+1):
                row = np.zeros(n-1 + n)
                # x_{i-1}
                if i-1 >= 1:
                    row[i-2] = 1
                # x_i
                if i <= n-1:
                    row[i-1] = 1
                # y_i coefficient -2
                row[n-1 + (i-1)] = -2
                A_eq.append(row)
                b_eq.append(p[i])
            # inequality constraints: x_{i-1}+x_i >= L_i
            A_ub = []
            b_ub = []
            for i in range(1, n+1):
                row = np.zeros(n-1 + n)
                if i-1 >= 1:
                    row[i-2] = -1
                if i <= n-1:
                    row[i-1] = -1
                # - (x_{i-1}+x_i) <= -L_i
                A_ub.append(row)
                b_ub.append(-L[i])
            # bounds: x_i >=0, y_i >=0
            bounds = [(0, None)]*(n-1 + n)
            # Use milp
            try:
                res = opt.milp(c=c, constraints=opt.LinearConstraint(np.vstack(A_eq), b_eq, b_eq),
                               bounds=bounds,
                               integrality=np.arange(n-1 + n),  # all variables integer
                               options={'disp': False})
            except Exception as e:
                # fallback maybe not supported
                # print("MILP error", e)
                continue
            if not res.success:
                continue
            total_x = sum(res.x[:n-1])
            L_moves = int(total_x) + 1
            if best is None or L_moves < best:
                best = L_moves
                best_info = (start, end, total_x, res.x[:n-1])
    return best, best_info

print(min_moves_path(5))

[CODE] (turn 6):
def min_moves_dp(n, start, end):
    # compute s_e
    s_e = [0]*(n+1)
    if start == end:
        s_e[start] = 2
    else:
        s_e[start] = 1
        s_e[end] = 1
    # compute L_i (lower bound) and p_i (parity)
    L = [0]*(n+1)
    p = [0]*(n+1)
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        p[i] = s_e[i] % 2
        if s_e[i]==0:
            if i%2==1:
                L[i] = 2
            else:
                L[i] = 0
        elif s_e[i]==1:
            if i%2==1:
                L[i] = 1
            else:
                L[i] = 3
        else:  # s_e[i]==2
            if i%2==1:
                L[i] = 0
            else:
                L[i] = 2
        # adjust L to have same parity as p (should already)
        if (L[i] - p[i]) % 2 != 0:
            L[i] += 1
    # DP
    # state: dict of x_{i-1} -> min sum of x_1..x_{i-2}
    dp = {0: 0}
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        newdp = {}
        for prev_x, total in dp.items():
            # need to choose x_i >=0 such that d = prev_x + x_i satisfies constraints
            # d >= L[i] and d ≡ p[i] (mod 2)
            # So x_i must be >= max(0, L[i] - prev_x) and have parity: (prev_x + x_i) %2 == p[i]
            min_x = max(0, L[i] - prev_x)
            # adjust min_x to satisfy parity
            if (prev_x + min_x) % 2 != p[i]:
                min_x += 1
            # Now any x_i = min_x + 2k (k>=0) is feasible
            # Since we want minimal total sum, we will consider only min_x (no extra)
            x_i = min_x
            new_total = total + x_i
            # store minimal total for this x_i
            if x_i not in newdp or new_total < newdp[x_i]:
                newdp[x_i] = new_total
        dp = newdp
    # after processing i=n, we have x_n as key
    if 0 in dp:
        total_x = dp[0]
        L_moves = total_x + 1
        return L_moves
    else:
        return None

def min_moves_bruteforce_n(n):
    best = None
    best_pair = None
    for start in range(1, n+1):
        for end in range(start, n+1):
            moves = min_moves_dp(n, start, end)
            if moves is not None:
                if best is None or moves < best:
                    best = moves
                    best_pair = (start, end)
    return best, best_pair

for n in range(1, 11):
    best, pair = min_moves_bruteforce_n(n)
    print(n, best, pair)

[CODE] (turn 8):
def reconstruct_solution(n, start, end):
    s_e = [0]*(n+1)
    if start == end:
        s_e[start] = 2
    else:
        s_e[start] = 1
        s_e[end] = 1
    L = [0]*(n+1)
    p = [0]*(n+1)
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        p[i] = s_e[i] % 2
        if s_e[i]==0:
            L[i] = 2 if i%2==1 else 0
        elif s_e[i]==1:
            L[i] = 1 if i%2==1 else 3
        else:
            L[i] = 0 if i%2==1 else 2
        if (L[i] - p[i]) % 2 != 0:
            L[i] += 1
    # DP with backpointers
    dp = {0: (0, None)}  # x_{i-1}: (total sum up to previous, prev_x)
    # We'll also store predecessor mapping for each step
    prev_map = [{} for _ in range(n+1)]  # prev_map[i][x_i] = (prev_x, total)
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        newdp = {}
        for prev_x, (total, _) in dp.items():
            min_x = max(0, L[i] - prev_x)
            if (prev_x + min_x) % 2 != p[i]:
                min_x += 1
            x_i = min_x
            new_total = total + x_i
            if x_i not in newdp or new_total < newdp[x_i][0]:
                newdp[x_i] = (new_total, prev_x)
        dp = newdp
        prev_map[i] = dp
    # find x_n = 0
    if 0 not in dp:
        return None
    # backtrack
    x_vals = [0]*(n+1)  # x_0..x_n
    x_vals[n] = 0
    cur = 0
    for i in range(n, 0, -1):
        total, prev_x = dp[cur]
        x_vals[i-1] = prev_x
        cur = prev_x
        # need to retrieve dp for previous i-1: we stored dp for each i? Actually dp variable currently holds mapping for i. We'll need to store all dp states.
    # Not straightforward; let's recompute with storing predecessor.

def reconstruct_solution(n, start, end):

    else:
        s_e[start] = 1

[CODE] (turn 9):
def dp_solution(n, start, end):
    s_e = [0]*(n+1)
    if start == end:
        s_e[start] = 2
    else:
        s_e[start] = 1
        s_e[end] = 1
    L = [0]*(n+1)
    p = [0]*(n+1)
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        p[i] = s_e[i] % 2
        if s_e[i]==0:
            L[i] = 2 if i%2==1 else 0
        elif s_e[i]==1:
            L[i] = 1 if i%2==1 else 3
        else:
            L[i] = 0 if i%2==1 else 2
        if (L[i] - p[i]) % 2 != 0:
            L[i] += 1
    # DP tables: dp[i][x_i] = (total_sum, prev_x)
    dp = [{} for _ in range(n+1)]
    dp[0][0] = (0, None)  # x_0 = 0
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        for prev_x, (total, _) in dp[i-1].items():
            # choose x_i
            min_x = max(0, L[i] - prev_x)
            if (prev_x + min_x) % 2 != p[i]:
                min_x += 1
            x_i = min_x
            new_total = total + x_i
            # store best for x_i
            if x_i not in dp[i] or new_total < dp[i][x_i][0]:
                dp[i][x_i] = (new_total, prev_x)
    # need x_n = 0
    if 0 not in dp[n]:
        return None
    # reconstruct x values
    x_vals = [0]*(n+1)
    cur = 0
    for i in range(n, 0, -1):
        total, prev_x = dp[i][cur]
        x_vals[i-1] = prev_x
        cur = prev_x
    # x_vals[0]=0, x_vals[n]=0 automatically
    total_x = dp[n][0][0]
    moves = total_x + 1
    return moves, x_vals

# test for n=5, start=1, end=5
print(dp_solution(5,1,5))

[CODE] (turn 9):
def dp_solution(n, start, end):
    s_e = [0]*(n+1)
    if start == end:
        s_e[start] = 2
    else:
        s_e[start] = 1
        s_e[end] = 1
    L = [0]*(n+1)
    p = [0]*(n+1)
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        p[i] = s_e[i] % 2
        if s_e[i]==0:
            L[i] = 2 if i%2==1 else 0
        elif s_e[i]==1:
            L[i] = 1 if i%2==1 else 3
        else:
            L[i] = 0 if i%2==1 else 2
        if (L[i] - p[i]) % 2 != 0:
            L[i] += 1
    # DP tables: dp[i][x_i] = (total_sum, prev_x)
    dp = [{} for _ in range(n+1)]
    dp[0][0] = (0, None)  # x_0 = 0
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        for prev_x, (total, _) in dp[i-1].items():
            # choose x_i
            min_x = max(0, L[i] - prev_x)
            if (prev_x + min_x) % 2 != p[i]:
                min_x += 1
            x_i = min_x
            new_total = total + x_i
            # store best for x_i
            if x_i not in dp[i] or new_total < dp[i][x_i][0]:
                dp[i][x_i] = (new_total, prev_x)
    # need x_n = 0
    if 0 not in dp[n]:
        return None
    # reconstruct x values
    x_vals = [0]*(n+1)
    cur = 0
    for i in range(n, 0, -1):
        total, prev_x = dp[i][cur]
        x_vals[i-1] = prev_x
        cur = prev_x
    # x_vals[0]=0, x_vals[n]=0 automatically
    total_x = dp[n][0][0]
    moves = total_x + 1
    return moves, x_vals

# test for n=5, start=1, end=5
print(dp_solution(5,1,5))

[CODE] (turn 10):
def min_moves_dp_mod4(n, start, end):
    s_e = [0]*(n+1)
    if start == end:
        s_e[start] = 2
    else:
        s_e[start] = 1
        s_e[end] = 1
    L = [0]*(n+1)
    r = [0]*(n+1)
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        if s_e[i]==0:
            if i%2==1:
                L[i] = 2
                r[i] = 2
            else:
                L[i] = 0
                r[i] = 0
        elif s_e[i]==1:
            if i%2==1:
                L[i] = 1
                r[i] = 1
            else:
                L[i] = 3
                r[i] = 3
        else:  # s_e[i]==2
            if i%2==1:
                L[i] = 0
                r[i] = 0
            else:
                L[i] = 2
                r[i] = 2
    # DP
    dp = {0: 0}  # x_{i-1} -> total sum up to previous edge
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        newdp = {}
        for prev_x, total in dp.items():
            # need d = prev_x + x_i
            min_d = max(L[i], prev_x)  # because x_i >=0 => d >= prev_x
            # adjust min_d to satisfy congruence d ≡ r[i] (mod 4)
            mod = (min_d - r[i]) % 4
            if mod != 0:
                min_d += (4 - mod)
            # Now d >= min_d and d ≡ r[i] (mod 4). The minimal is min_d.
            d = min_d
            x_i = d - prev_x
            new_total = total + x_i
            # store minimal total for this x_i
            if x_i not in newdp or new_total < newdp[x_i]:
                newdp[x_i] = new_total
        dp = newdp
    # need x_n = 0
    if 0 in dp:
        total_x = dp[0]
        moves = total_x + 1
        return moves
    else:
        return None

def min_moves_bruteforce_mod4(n):
    best = None
    best_pair = None
    for start in range(1, n+1):
        for end in range(start, n+1):
            moves = min_moves_dp_mod4(n, start, end)
            if moves is not None:
                if best is None or moves < best:
                    best = moves
                    best_pair = (start, end)
    return best, best_pair

for n in range(1, 13):
    best, pair = min_moves_bruteforce_mod4(n)
    print(n, best, pair)

def formula(n):
    r = n % 4
    if r in (0,1):
        return n
    elif r == 2:
        return n-1
    else:  # r==3
        return n+1

    print(n, best, formula(n), best==formula(n))

def reconstruct_solution_mod4(n, start, end):
    s_e = [0]*(n+1)
    if start == end:
        s_e[start] = 2
    else:
        s_e[start] = 1
        s_e[end] = 1
    L = [0]*(n+1)
    r = [0]*(n+1)
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        if s_e[i]==0:
            if i%2==1:
                L[i]=2; r[i]=2
            else:
                L[i]=0; r[i]=0
        elif s_e[i]==1:
            if i%2==1:
                L[i]=1; r[i]=1
            else:
                L[i]=3; r[i]=3
        else:
            if i%2==1:
                L[i]=0; r[i]=0
            else:
                L[i]=2; r[i]=2
    # DP with backtracking storing predecessor
    dp = [{} for _ in range(n+1)]
    dp[0][0] = (0, None)  # total sum, prev_x
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        for prev_x, (total, _) in dp[i-1].items():
            min_d = max(L[i], prev_x)
            # adjust to satisfy mod 4
            mod = (min_d - r[i]) % 4
            if mod != 0:
                min_d += (4 - mod)
            d = min_d
            x_i = d - prev_x
            new_total = total + x_i
            if x_i not in dp[i] or new_total < dp[i][x_i][0]:
                dp[i][x_i] = (new_total, prev_x)
    if 0 not in dp[n]:
        return None
    # backtrack
    x_vals = [0]*(n+1)
    cur = 0
    for i in range(n, 0, -1):
        total, prev_x = dp[i][cur]
        x_vals[i-1] = prev_x
        cur = prev_x
    total_x = dp[n][0][0]
    moves = total_x + 1
    return moves, x_vals

print(reconstruct_solution_mod4(5,1,1))

def compute_f_from_x(n, x_vals, start, end):
    f = [0]*(n+1)
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        s_e = 0
        if start == end and i == start:
            s_e = 2
        else:
            if i == start or i == end:
                s_e = 1
        f[i] = (x_vals[i-1] + x_vals[i] + s_e)//2
    return f[1:]

moves, x_vals = reconstruct_solution_mod4(5,1,1)
f = compute_f_from_x(5, x_vals, 1,1)
print("x:", x_vals)
print("f:", f)

[CODE] (turn 15):
from collections import deque

def bfs_min_moves(n):
    # initial state: alternating T/H starting with T at position 1.
    # Represent state as bitmask of heads (1) vs tails (0). Let's define 1 = heads, 0 = tails.
    # Initial: position i odd => 0 (tails), even => 1 (heads)
    init = 0
    for i in range(n):
        if (i+1)%2==0:  # even position -> heads (1)
            init |= (1<<i)
    # goal: all heads => mask all 1s
    goal = (1<<n)-1
    # BFS over (mask, pos) where pos is index of last flipped coin (1-indexed)
    # First move can be any coin, so we start with each possible first flip.
    visited = set()
    q = deque()
    # initial moves: flip any coin i
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        mask = init ^ (1<<(i-1))  # flip
        state = (mask, i)
        visited.add(state)
        q.append((mask, i, 1))
    while q:
        mask, pos, steps = q.popleft()
        if mask == goal:
            return steps
        # next moves: flip adjacent coin
        for nxt in (pos-1, pos+1):
            if 1 <= nxt <= n:
                newmask = mask ^ (1<<(nxt-1))
                state = (newmask, nxt)
                if state not in visited:
                    visited.add(state)
                    q.append((newmask, nxt, steps+1))
    return None

for n in range(1, 9):
    print(n, bfs_min_moves(n))

from collections import deque

def bfs_min_moves(n):
    # initial state: alternating T/H starting with T at position 1.
    # Represent state as bitmask of heads (1) vs tails (0). Let's define 1 = heads, 0 = tails.
    # Initial: position i odd => 0 (tails), even => 1 (heads)

        visited.add(state)

[CODE] (turn 1):
from collections import deque

def min_moves(n):
    # n coins, alternating T (tails) at odd positions, H at even.
    # Represent state as bitmask of length n: 1 for heads, 0 for tails? Let's use 1=heads, 0=tails.
    # initial: odd positions tails (0), even heads (1)
    init = 0
    for i in range(n):
        if (i+1)%2==0:  # even position heads
            init |= (1<<i)
    target = (1<<n)-1  # all heads
    # BFS over (state, last flipped index) where last index = -1 for first move (any)
    from collections import deque
    visited = {}
    q = deque()
    # first move any coin
    for i in range(n):
        new_state = init ^ (1<<i)  # flip
        visited[(new_state,i)] = 1
        q.append((new_state,i,1))
    while q:
        state,last,dist = q.popleft()
        if state==target:
            return dist
        # next moves: flip adjacent to last
        for nb in [last-1,last+1]:
            if 0<=nb<n:
                new_state = state ^ (1<<nb)
                key=(new_state,nb)
                if key not in visited:
                    visited[key]=dist+1
                    q.append((new_state,nb,dist+1))
    return None

for n in range(1,11):
    print(n, min_moves(n))

[CODE] (turn 2):
def min_moves_bruteforce(n,limit=1000000):
    # BFS may explode for n>10; we can use BFS with pruning maybe up to n=12.
    from collections import deque
    init = 0
    for i in range(n):
        if (i+1)%2==0:
            init |= (1<<i)
    target = (1<<n)-1
    visited = set()
    q = deque()
    for i in range(n):
        state = init ^ (1<<i)
        q.append((state,i,1))
        visited.add((state,i))
    while q:
        state,last,dist = q.popleft()
        if state==target:
            return dist
        for nb in (last-1,last+1):
            if 0<=nb<n:
                new_state = state ^ (1<<nb)
                key=(new_state,nb)
                if key not in visited:
                    visited.add(key)
                    q.append((new_state,nb,dist+1))
    return None

for n in range(2,13,2):
    print(n, min_moves_bruteforce(n))

import itertools, math, sys, numpy as np, scipy.optimize as opt, scipy.sparse as sp

def min_moves_ilp(n):
    # variables: start_i (binary), x_i (nonneg integer), y_i (nonneg integer)
    # We'll use integer variables (nonnegative) but we can restrict to small values.
    # We'll use linear constraints.
    # Number of variables: n start + 2*(n-1) arcs.
    N_start = n
    N_edges = n-1
    # variable ordering: start[0..n-1], x[0..n-2], y[0..n-2]
    total_vars = N_start + 2*N_edges
    # objective: minimize sum x_i + y_i
    c = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
    c[N_start:] = 1  # x and y have coefficient 1
    # constraints:
    # 1) exactly one start
    A_eq = []
    b_eq = []
    row = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
    row[:N_start] = 1
    A_eq.append(row)
    b_eq.append(1)
    # 2) parity constraints: start_i + indegree_i ≡ p_i (mod 2)
    # We'll enforce parity via equality with auxiliary binary variables? Hard.
    # Instead we can enforce exact parity by requiring start_i + indegree_i = p_i + 2*z_i where z_i integer >=0.
    # Introduce integer slack variable z_i (nonnegative integer). We'll add them to model.
    # Let's add extra variables for each vertex.
    # We'll expand variable list to include z_i.
    # For simplicity, we can brute force start position and solve linear program for each start.
    best = None
    for s in range(n):
        # set start_i = 1 for i=s
        # Build constraints for indegree parity: indegree_i = p_i - start_i + 2*z_i
        # We'll treat z_i as integer variable.
        # We'll create variables: x_i, y_i, z_i (n of them)
        # total vars = 2*(n-1) + n
        N_z = n
        total = 2*N_edges + N_z
        c = np.zeros(total, dtype=int)
        c[:2*N_edges] = 1  # x and y cost
        # Build equality constraints:
        A_eq = []
        b_eq = []
        # indegree_i = x_{i-1} + y_i (with x_{-1}=0, y_{n-1}=0)
        for i in range(n):
            row = np.zeros(total, dtype=int)
            # x_{i-1}
            if i-1 >= 0:
                row[i-1] = 1
            # y_i
            if i < n-1:
                row[N_edges + i] = 1
            # z_i coefficient -2
            row[2*N_edges + i] = -2
            # RHS = p_i - start_i
            p = 1 if (i+1)%2==1 else 0
            start_i = 1 if i==s else 0
            b = p - start_i
            A_eq.append(row)
            b_eq.append(b)
        # Additional constraints: flow balance (Eulerian trail) for interior vertices:
        # outdegree_i - indegree_i = delta_i where delta_i = 1 for start, -1 for end, 0 otherwise.
        #

import itertools, math, sys, numpy as np, scipy.optimize as opt, scipy.sparse as sp

def min_moves_ilp(n):
    # variables: start_i (binary), x_i (nonneg integer), y_i (nonneg integer)
    # We'll use integer variables (nonnegative) but we can restrict to small values.
    # We'll use linear constraints.
    # Number of variables: n start + 2*(n-1) arcs.
    N_start = n
    N_edges = n-1
    # variable ordering: start[0..n-1], x[0..n-2], y[0..n-2]
    total_vars = N_start + 2*N_edges
    # objective: minimize sum x_i + y_i
    c = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
    c[N_start:] = 1  # x and y have coefficient 1
    # constraints:
    # 1) exactly one start
    A_eq = []
    b_eq = []
    row = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
    row[:N_start] = 1
    A_eq.append(row)
    b_eq.append(1)
    # 2) parity constraints: start_i + indegree_i ≡ p_i (mod 2)
    # We'll enforce parity via equality with auxiliary binary variables? Hard.
    # Instead we can enforce exact parity by requiring start_i + indegree_i = p_i + 2*z_i where z_i integer >=0.
    # Introduce integer slack variable z_i (nonnegative integer). We'll add them to model.
    # Let's add extra variables for each vertex.
    # We'll expand variable list to include z_i.
    # For simplicity, we can brute force start position and solve linear program for each start.
    best = None
    for s in range(n):
        # set start_i = 1 for i=s
        # Build constraints for indegree parity: indegree_i = p_i - start_i + 2*z_i
        # We'll treat z_i as integer variable.
        # We'll create variables: x_i, y_i, z_i (n of them)
        # total vars = 2*(n-1) + n
        N_z = n
        total = 2*N_edges + N_z
        c = np.zeros(total, dtype=int)
        c[:2*N_edges] = 1  # x and y cost
        # Build equality constraints:
        A_eq = []
        b_eq = []
        # indegree_i = x_{i-1} + y_i (with x_{-1}=0, y_{n-1}=0)
        for i in range(n):
            row = np.zeros(total, dtype=int)
            # x_{i-1}
            if i-1 >= 0:
                row[i-1] = 1
            # y_i
            if i < n-1:
                row[N_edges + i] = 1
            # z_i coefficient -2
            row[2*N_edges + i] = -2
            # RHS = p_i - start_i
            p = 1 if (i+1)%2==1 else 0
            start_i = 1 if i==s else 0
            b = p - start_i
            A_eq.append(row)
            b_eq.append(b)
        # Additional constraints: flow balance (Eulerian trail) for interior vertices:
        # outdegree_i - indegree_i = delta_i where delta_i = 1 for start, -1 for end, 0 otherwise.
        #

[CODE] (turn 2):
def min_moves(n):
    # n odd length, alternating starting with T at position 0 (index 0)
    target = tuple(1 if i%2==0 else 0 for i in range(n))  # need odd positions (0-index even) visited odd times
    # BFS over (pos, parity_vector) where parity_vector is tuple of bits visited mod2 so far.
    from collections import deque
    start_states = []
    # first move can be any coin: we flip it, so parity vector has 1 at that position.
    for i in range(n):
        vec = [0]*n
        vec[i] = 1
        start_states.append((i, tuple(vec)))
    # BFS
    visited = set(start_states)
    dq = deque()
    for state in start_states:
        dq.append((state, 1))  # moves count = 1
    while dq:
        (pos, vec), steps = dq.popleft()
        if vec == target:
            return steps
        # next moves: flip adjacent coin
        for nxt in (pos-1, pos+1):
            if 0 <= nxt < n:
                new_vec = list(vec)
                new_vec[nxt] ^= 1
                new_state = (nxt, tuple(new_vec))
                if new_state not in visited:
                    visited.add(new_state)
                    dq.append((new_state, steps+1))
    return None

for n in range(1, 13, 2):
    print(n, min_moves(n))

def min_moves_limit(n, max_states=5_000_000):
    target = tuple(1 if i%2==0 else 0 for i in range(n))
    from collections import deque
    start_states = []
    for i in range(n):
        vec = [0]*n
        vec[i] = 1
        start_states.append((i, tuple(vec)))
    visited = set(start_states)
    dq = deque()
    for state in start_states:
        dq.append((state, 1))
    while dq:
        (pos, vec), steps = dq.popleft()
        if vec == target:
            return steps
        for nxt in (pos-1, pos+1):
            if 0 <= nxt < n:
                new_vec = list(vec)
                new_vec[nxt] ^= 1
                new_state = (nxt, tuple(new_vec))
                if new_state not in visited:
                    visited.add(new_state)
                    dq.append((new_state, steps+1))
        if len(visited) > max_states:
            return None

for n in range(21, 27, 2):
    print(n, min_moves_limit(n))

def min_moves_limit(n, max_states=5_000_000):

    start_states = []
    for i in range(n):

        vec[i] = 1
        start_states.append((i, tuple(vec)))
    visited = set(start_states)
    dq = deque()
    for state in start_states:
        dq.append((state, 1))
    while dq:
        (pos, vec), steps = dq.popleft()
        if vec == target:
            return steps
        for nxt in (pos-1, pos+1):
            if 0 <= nxt < n:
                new_vec = list(vec)
                new_vec[nxt] ^= 1
                new_state = (nxt, tuple(new_vec))
                if new_state not in visited:
                    visited.add(new_state)

def min_moves_bitmask(n):
    target = 0
    for i in range(n):
        if i%2==0:  # odd position (1-index) => index 0 even
            target |= (1<<i)
    from collections import deque
    visited = set()
    dq = deque()
    for i in range(n):
        mask = 1<<i
        state = (i, mask)
        visited.add(state)
        dq.append((state, 1))
    while dq:
        (pos, mask), steps = dq.popleft()
        if mask == target:
            return steps
        for nxt in (pos-1, pos+1):
            if 0 <= nxt < n:
                new_mask = mask ^ (1<<nxt)
                new_state = (nxt, new_mask)
                if new_state not in visited:
                    visited.add(new_state)
                    dq.append((new_state, steps+1))
    return None

for n in range(1, 23, 2):
    print(n, min_moves_bitmask(n))

The formula derived from exhaustive search for small n suggests minimal moves follow pattern 4k - 1 for even k, 4k for odd k. This seems plausible and can be proven.

Let's attempt to prove.

We can think of the walk as sequence of moves. Let’s denote the number of times we traverse edge (i,i+1) in each direction. Let a_i = number of traversals from i to i+1, b_i = number from i+1 to i. For each vertex i, the number of arrivals (visits) v_i = (1 if start = i else 0) + a_{i-1} + b_i (incoming from left and right). Similarly departures = a_i + b_{i-1}.

We need parity constraints: v_i ≡ p_i (mod 2) where p_i = 1 for odd i (1-index) else 0.

Also we have start and end constraints: there is exactly one vertex s where out - in = 1 (start), one vertex t where in - out = 1 (end), others equal.

Goal: minimize total moves = sum_i v_i = 1 + total traversals (since each traversal adds one arrival). So minimizing sum_i v_i is equivalent to minimizing total traversals.

We can formulate linear programming integer problem: minimize sum_i (a_i + b_i) subject to parity constraints and flow constraints.

Because graph is a path, we can solve analytically.

Let’s define net flow f_i = a_i - b_i (net traversals from i to i+1). Then for each vertex i, the difference between out and in is:

out_i - in_i = (a_i + b_{i-1}) - (a_{i-1} + b_i) = (a_i - b_i) - (a_{i-1} - b_{i-1}) = f_i - f_{i-1}.

Thus out_i - in_i = f_i - f_{i-1}.

For start s, out - in = 1. For end t, out - in = -1. For others 0.

Thus we have f_i - f_{i-1} = δ_i where δ_i = 1 at i = s, -1 at i = t, 0 otherwise. This implies f_i is piecewise constant except jumps at s and t. Since path indices from 1 to n-1 (edges). So f_i is constant except at positions s and t.

Thus f_i is some integer constant C for edges before s, then C+1 after s up to t-1, then C after t (if s < t). Or if s > t, similar.

Thus net flow f_i takes at most two values differing by 1.

Now total traversals on edge i is a_i + b_i = |f_i| + 2 * min(a_i,b_i). Since a_i + b_i >= |f_i|, with equality when traversals are all in same direction (no back-and-forth). To minimize total traversals, we would like to avoid unnecessary back-and-forth, i.e., set min(a_i,b_i) as small as possible, ideally 0, so a_i or b_i = 0, i.e., all traversals on each edge go same direction. However parity constraints on arrivals may force some back-and-forth.

Now arrivals parity: v_i = (1 if i = start) + a_{i-1} + b_i. Since a_{i-1} + b_i = (|f_{i-1}| + |f_i|)/2 + something? Not straightforward.

But perhaps we can find minimal solution using pattern: start at leftmost odd (position 1) and end at rightmost odd (position n). Then s=1, t=n. Then f_i = 1 for edges i from 1 to n-1? Let's compute: For start at 1 (vertex index 1) and end at n (odd). Then out - in at 1 = 1, at n = -1. So δ_1 = 1, δ_n = -1. Then f_i - f_{i-1} = δ_i. So f_1 = 1 (since f_0 = 0). For i between 2 and n-1, δ_i = 0, so f_i = f_{i-1} = 1. For i = n, not defined (edge index up to n-1). So f_i = 1 for all edges. That means net flow of 1 from left to right across each edge. So we need to traverse each edge at least once in direction left->right. To achieve net flow 1, we could simply traverse each edge exactly once left->right, i.e., a_i = 1, b_i = 0. That would give total traversals = n-1 = 7872. Then arrivals: compute v_i = (1 if i=1) + a_{i-1} + b_i = (1 if i=1) + a_{i-1}. Since b_i = 0. So for i=1: v_1 = 1 (start) + a_0? a_0 not defined, treat as 0. So v_1 = 1. For i>1: v_i = a_{i-1} = 1 (since each a_{i-1}=1). So all vertices visited exactly once. This yields parity v_i = 1 for all i, which is not correct (even vertices need 0). So this fails parity.

Thus we need to add extra traversals to adjust parity of even vertices.

Observation: Since each even vertex currently visited once (odd), we need to add an extra visit to each even vertex to make it even (i.e., add one more arrival). Adding an extra arrival to an even vertex can be done by traversing an adjacent edge in opposite direction (i.e., backtrack). For each even vertex i (even index), we can add a back-and-forth on one of its incident edges to increase its arrival count by 1 (or 2?). Let's analyze.

If we add a traversal from i to i-1 (i.e., b_{i-1} increase by 1) and later a traversal from i-1 to i (increase a_{i-1} by 1), that adds 2 traversals (a round trip) and adds 1 extra arrival to i (when we go from i-1 to i) and also adds 1 extra arrival to i-1 (when we go from i to i-1). So each such back-and-forth adds 2 arrivals (one to each endpoint). Since we need to add one extra arrival to each even vertex (to make it even), we could pair evens with neighboring odds to add extra arrivals to both. But odds already have odd parity (1) and need to stay odd, so adding 1 extra arrival to an odd would make it even (bad). So we must add arrivals to evens in pairs such that odds get even number of extra arrivals (i.e., 0 or 2). So we can add a back-and-forth on edge between even i and odd i-1 (left) and also another back-and-forth on edge between even i and odd i+1 (right) to give each odd two extra arrivals (keeping parity odd). So each pair adds one extra arrival to each endpoint.

Thus each back-and-forth pair contributes +1 arrival to both vertices of that edge.

Thus the parity condition can be expressed: Starting from base walk (simple path from start to end) where each vertex visited once, we need to add some number of back-and-forth pairs on edges to adjust arrivals parity. Let x_i be number of back-and-forth pairs on edge i (i from 1 to n-1). Then total extra arrivals to vertex i are:

- For interior vertex i (2..n-1): extra arrivals = x_{i-1} + x_i (since each pair on left edge adds arrival to i when traversed from i-1 to i, and each pair on right edge adds arrival to i when traversed from i+1 to i). For endpoints: vertex 1 extra arrivals = x_1 (since only edge 1). Vertex n extra arrivals = x_{n-1}.

Base arrivals = 1 for each vertex. Desired parity: For odd vertices (positions 1,3,5,...), total arrivals must be odd => extra arrivals must be even (0,2,4...). For even vertices, total arrivals must be even => extra arrivals must be odd (1,3,5...). So we need to choose nonnegative integers x_i such that:

- For odd i: x_{i-1} + x_i (with x_0 = 0, x_n = 0) is even.
- For even i: x_{i-1} + x_i is odd.

Goal: minimize total extra traversals = 2 * sum_i x_i (since each pair adds 2 traversals). Equivalent to minimize sum_i x_i.

Thus we have a linear integer optimization problem on a path.

Let’s define y_i = x_i mod 2 (parity of x_i). Since we care about parity constraints, we can first find minimal sum of x_i given parity constraints, then we can set x_i to be either 0 or 1 (since adding 2 to any x_i increases sum by 2, which would increase total moves by 4, not optimal). So optimal solution will have each x_i either 0 or 1 (since any larger value would increase sum unnecessarily). So we need to find a 0-1 assignment to edges such that for each vertex i, parity condition holds: for odd i, x_{i-1} + x_i even; for even i, x_{i-1} + x_i odd.

This is a system of equations mod 2 on a path. Let's solve.

Let’s index vertices 1..n. Define x_0 = x_n = 0 (since no edge outside). For i from 1 to n, we have condition:

If i odd: x_{i-1} + x_i ≡ 0 (mod 2)
If i even: x_{i-1} + x_i ≡ 1 (mod 2)

These are linear equations mod 2. Let's solve for x_i.

We can treat recurrence: given x_{i-1}, we can determine x_i.

For i=1 (odd): x_0 + x_1 ≡ 0 => 0 + x_1 ≡ 0 => x_1 = 0.

Thus x_1 = 0.

Now i=2 (even): x_1 + x_2 ≡ 1 => 0 + x_2 = 1 => x_2 = 1.

i=3 (odd): x_2 + x_3 ≡ 0 => 1 + x_3 = 0 => x_3 = 1 (since 1+1=0 mod2). So x_3 = 1.

i=4 (even): x_3 + x_4 = 1 => 1 + x_4 = 1 => x_4 = 0.

i=5 (odd): x_4 + x_5 = 0 => 0 + x_5 = 0 => x_5 = 0.

i=6 (even): x_5 + x_6 = 1 => 0 + x_6 = 1 => x_6 = 1.

Pattern emerges: x_i repeats every 4? Let's compute sequence for edges i=1..n-1.

We have: x1=0, x2=1, x3=1, x4=0, x5=0, x6=1, x7=1, x8=0,...

Thus pattern of length 4: [0,1,1,0] repeating.

Now we must also satisfy final condition at i=n (odd, since n is odd). For i=n (odd): x_{n-1} + x_n = 0, with x_n = 0. So we need x_{n-1} = 0. Since pattern repeats every 4, we need x_{n-1}=0. Let's check n odd: n = 2k+1, so n-1 = 2k, which is even. Edge index n-1 is even. According to pattern, edges with index ≡ 2 mod 4 have value 1, edges with index ≡ 0 mod 4 have value 0. Let's compute: n-1 = 2k. If k even? Let's compute parity: 2k mod 4 = 0 if k even, 2 if k odd. So x_{n-1}=0 if k even, =1 if k odd. The condition requires x_{n-1}=0, thus k must be even. from collections import deque

def min_moves(n):
    # initial pattern: alternating tails (0) and heads (1), leftmost tails (0)
    init = tuple(0 if i%2==0 else 1 for i in range(n))  # 0-indexed, position 0 tails
    target = tuple([1]*n)
    # BFS over (bits, pos) where pos is index of last flipped coin (0..n-1)
    # initial moves: any position can be first flipped
    # We'll push all possibilities with cost 1
    visited = {}
    q = deque()
    for pos in range(n):
        bits = list(init)
        bits[pos] ^= 1  # flip
        bits = tuple(bits)
        state = (bits, pos)
        visited[state] = 1
        q.append(state)
        if bits == target:
            return 1
    while q:
        bits, pos = q.popleft()
        steps = visited[(bits,pos)]
        # next moves: flip adjacent coin
        for np in [pos-1, pos+1]:
            if 0 <= np < n:
                newbits = list(bits)
                newbits[np] ^= 1
                newbits = tuple(newbits)
                newstate = (newbits, np)
                if newstate not in visited:
                    visited[newstate] = steps+1
                    if newbits == target:
                        return steps+1
                    q.append(newstate)
    return None

for n in range(1,15,2):
    print(n, min_moves(n))

    target = tuple([1]*n)
    from collections import deque
    q = deque()

    # initial states: any first flip
    for pos in range(n):
        bits = list(init)
        bits[pos] ^= 1
        bits = tuple(bits)
        state = (bits, pos)
        visited[state] = 1
        q.append(state)
        if bits == target:
            return 1
    while q:
        bits, pos = q.popleft()
        steps = visited[(bits,pos)]
        for np in (pos-1, pos+1):
            if 0 <= np < n:
                newbits = list(bits)
                newbits[np] ^= 1
                newbits = tuple(newbits)
                newstate = (newbits, np)
                if newstate not in visited:
                    visited[newstate] = steps+1
                    if newbits == target:

def find_path(n):
    init = tuple(0 if i%2==0 else 1 for i in range(n))
    target = tuple([1]*n)
    from collections import deque
    q = deque()
    visited = {}
    parent = {}
    for pos in range(n):
        bits = list(init)
        bits[pos] ^= 1
        bits = tuple(bits)
        state = (bits, pos)
        visited[state] = 1
        parent[state] = None
        q.append(state)
        if bits == target:
            return [state]
    while q:
        bits, pos = q.popleft()
        steps = visited[(bits,pos)]
        for np in (pos-1, pos+1):
            if 0 <= np < n:
                newbits = list(bits)
                newbits[np] ^= 1
                newbits = tuple(newbits)
                newstate = (newbits, np)
                if newstate not in visited:
                    visited[newstate] = steps+1
                    parent[newstate] = (bits, pos)
                    if newbits == target:
                        # reconstruct path
                        path = [newstate]
                        cur = (bits, pos)
                        while cur is not None:
                            path.append(cur)
                            cur = parent[cur]
                        path.reverse()
                        return path
                    q.append(newstate)
    return None

path5 = find_path(5)
len(path5)-1, path5[:10]

def min_moves_formula(n):
    # compute minimal moves using search over start s (any vertex) and end t (any vertex)
    # using parity toggles model (edge toggles cost 2 each)
    d = [1 if i%2==0 else 0 for i in range(n)]  # 0-indexed: odd positions (i even) are tails demand 1
    best = None
    for s in range(n):
        for t in range(n):
            # base path from s to t (simple path)
            # we assume walk goes from s to t directly without backtracking
            # arrivals parity p_i: vertices visited after start s.
            # For vertices on path (excluding s) we have arrival parity 1, others 0.
            # Path includes vertices between s and t inclusive.
            if s==t:
                p = [0]*n
            else:
                # direction doesn't matter for parity; vertices between s and t inclusive, excluding s
                lo, hi = min(s,t), max(s,t)
                p = [0]*n
                for i in range(lo+1, hi+1):
                    p[i] = 1
            # compute difference vector q = d xor p
            q = [(d[i] ^ p[i]) for i in range(n)]
            # need to represent q as sum of edge toggles (each toggles adjacent pair)
            # Minimum number of toggles needed equals (number of 1's in q)/2 after pairing adjacent? Not always.
            # This is equivalent to finding minimum number of edges whose incidence vector equals q (mod 2).
            # This is a linear system over GF(2). For a path graph, solution exists iff sum(q) even.
            # Minimum number of edges in solution equals (sum over i of prefix sum of q)?? Let's solve via DP.
            # For path, we can greedily pair: scan left to right, if q[i]==1, toggle edge (i,i+1) to fix it.
            # This yields a solution with number of toggles = number of 1's in q after processing? Let's compute.
            # Greedy: for i from 0 to n-2:
            #   if q[i]==1: toggle edge i,i+1 => flip q[i] and q[i+1]
            # At end, q[n-1] must be 0 for solvable.
            # Number of toggles = count of times we toggle.
            qq = q.copy()
            toggles = 0
            for i in range(n-1):
                if qq[i]==1:
                    # toggle edge i,i+1
                    qq[i] ^= 1
                    qq[i+1] ^= 1
                    toggles += 1
            if qq[-1]==1:
                # unsolvable
                continue
            total_moves = (abs(t-s) + 1) + 2*toggles  # distance+1 + 2*toggles
            if best is None or total_moves < best:
                best = total_moves
    return best

for n in range(1,21,2):
    print(n, min_moves_bruteforce(n), min_moves_formula(n))

[CODE] (turn 7):
def min_moves_formula(n):
    # compute minimal moves using search over start s (any vertex) and end t (any vertex)
    # using parity toggles model (edge toggles cost 2 each)
    d = [1 if i%2==0 else 0 for i in range(n)]  # 0-indexed: odd positions (i even) are tails demand 1
    best = None
    for s in range(n):
        for t in range(n):
            # base path from s to t (simple path)
            # we assume walk goes from s to t directly without backtracking
            # arrivals parity p_i: vertices visited after start s.
            # For vertices on path (excluding s) we have arrival parity 1, others 0.
            # Path includes vertices between s and t inclusive.
            if s==t:
                p = [0]*n
            else:
                # direction doesn't matter for parity; vertices between s and t inclusive, excluding s
                lo, hi = min(s,t), max(s,t)
                p = [0]*n
                for i in range(lo+1, hi+1):
                    p[i] = 1
            # compute difference vector q = d xor p
            q = [(d[i] ^ p[i]) for i in range(n)]
            # need to represent q as sum of edge toggles (each toggles adjacent pair)
            # Minimum number of toggles needed equals (number of 1's in q)/2 after pairing adjacent? Not always.
            # This is equivalent to finding minimum number of edges whose incidence vector equals q (mod 2).
            # This is a linear system over GF(2). For a path graph, solution exists iff sum(q) even.
            # Minimum number of edges in solution equals (sum over i of prefix sum of q)?? Let's solve via DP.
            # For path, we can greedily pair: scan left to right, if q[i]==1, toggle edge (i,i+1) to fix it.
            # This yields a solution with number of toggles = number of 1's in q after processing? Let's compute.
            # Greedy: for i from 0 to n-2:
            #   if q[i]==1: toggle edge i,i+1 => flip q[i] and q[i+1]
            # At end, q[n-1] must be 0 for solvable.
            # Number of toggles = count of times we toggle.
            qq = q.copy()
            toggles = 0
            for i in range(n-1):
                if qq[i]==1:
                    # toggle edge i,i+1
                    qq[i] ^= 1
                    qq[i+1] ^= 1
                    toggles += 1
            if qq[-1]==1:
                # unsolvable
                continue
            total_moves = (abs(t-s) + 1) + 2*toggles  # distance+1 + 2*toggles
            if best is None or total_moves < best:
                best = total_moves
    return best

for n in range(1,21,2):
    print(n, min_moves_bruteforce(n), min_moves_formula(n))

def min_moves_bruteforce(n):
    init = tuple(0 if i%2==0 else 1 for i in range(n))
    target = tuple([1]*n)
    from collections import deque
    q = deque()
    visited = {}
    for pos in range(n):
        bits = list(init)
        bits[pos] ^= 1
        bits = tuple(bits)
        state = (bits, pos)
        visited[state] = 1
        q.append(state)
        if bits == target:
            return 1
    while q:
        bits, pos = q.popleft()
        steps = visited[(bits,pos)]
        for np in (pos-1, pos+1):
            if 0 <= np < n:
                newbits = list(bits)
                newbits[np] ^= 1
                newbits = tuple(newbits)
                newstate = (newbits, np)
                if newstate not in visited:
                    visited[newstate] = steps+1
                    if newbits == target:
                        return steps+1
                    q.append(newstate)
    return None

for n in range(1,21,2):
    print(n, min_moves_bruteforce(n), min_moves_formula(n))

def min_moves_formula_correct(n):
    d = [1 if i%2==0 else 0 for i in range(n)]  # demand: 1 for tail (odd index 1-indexed)
    best = None
    for s in range(n):
        for t in range(n):
            # base path from s to t (simple)
            if s==t:
                p = [0]*n
                distance = 0
            else:
                lo, hi = min(s,t), max(s,t)
                p = [0]*n
                for i in range(lo+1, hi+1):
                    p[i] = 1
                distance = hi - lo
            # arrivals parity a_i = p_i (since arrivals = 1 for vertices visited after start)
            # need to adjust with edge toggles (round-trips) which add 1 to both endpoints.
            # We'll represent extra toggles vector x_i for edge i (between i and i+1), each toggles a_i and a_{i+1}
            # Let a = p initially.
            # We need final a_i satisfying:
            # For i != s: a_i ≡ d_i (mod 2)
            # For i == s: (a_i) ≡ d_i + 1 (mod 2)  because need arrivals parity = demand(s)+1 mod2? Wait earlier derived:
            # total visits = 1 + a_s must have parity d_s.
            # So a_s ≡ d_s - 1 (mod2) which is d_s +1 mod2.
            target = [d[i] for i in range(n)]
            target[s] = (d[s] + 1) % 2  # required parity for arrivals at start
            # Now we need to find edge toggles to turn a into target.
            # Let diff_i = a_i xor target_i (need to flip those bits).
            diff = [(p[i] ^ target[i]) for i in range(n)]
            # Solve using greedy as before (since path graph, system solvable iff sum(diff) even)
            # Greedy from left to right.
            toggles = 0
            cur = diff.copy()
            for i in range(n-1):
                if cur[i]==1:
                    # toggle edge i,i+1
                    cur[i] ^= 1
                    cur[i+1] ^= 1
                    toggles += 1
            if cur[-1]==1:
                continue  # unsolvable
            total_moves = distance + 1 + 2*toggles
            if best is None or total_moves < best:
                best = total_moves
    return best

for n in range(1,21,2):
    print(n, min_moves_bruteforce(n), min_moves_formula_correct(n))

def best_config(n):
    d = [1 if i%2==0 else 0 for i in range(n)]
    best = (10**9, None)
    for s in range(n):
        for t in range(n):
            if s==t:
                p = [0]*n
                distance = 0
            else:
                lo, hi = min(s,t), max(s,t)
                p = [0]*n
                for i in range(lo+1, hi+1):
                    p[i] = 1
                distance = hi - lo
            target = d.copy()
            target[s] = (d[s] + 1) % 2
            diff = [(p[i] ^ target[i]) for i in range(n)]
            cur = diff.copy()
            toggles = 0
            for i in range(n-1):
                if cur[i]==1:
                    cur[i] ^= 1
                    cur[i+1] ^= 1
                    toggles += 1
            if cur[-1]==1:
                continue
            total = distance + 1 + 2*toggles
            if total < best[0]:
                best = (total, (s,t,distance,toggles,p,diff))
    return best

                    cur[i] ^= 1
                    cur[i+1] ^= 1
                    toggles += 1
            if cur[-1]==1:
                continue
            total = distance + 1 + 2*toggles
            if total < best[0]:
                best = (total, (s,t,distance,toggles,p,diff))

So tails visited odd times (good). Heads visited twice (even). So total moves = sum counts = 2*heads + (odd tails counts). Heads count = 4 heads each visited twice => 8 moves. Tails count = 5 tails visited odd times: 1+1+3+1+1=7 moves. Total = 15 matches.

Thus pattern: each head visited exactly twice (even), each tail visited once except maybe one tail visited three times (to adjust parity for extra moves). That extra tail visited three times adds 2 extra moves beyond the baseline of each tail visited once (which would be 5 moves). Baseline moves = tails count (odd) + 2*heads = (n+1)/2 + 2*(n-1)/2 = (n+1)/2 + (n-1) = (3n)/2? Let's compute: (n+1)/2 + (n-1) = (n+1 + 2n -2)/2 = (3n -1)/2. For n=9, (3*9 -1)/2 = (27-1)/2=13. That's baseline 13 moves. Actual optimal is 15, which is +2. For n=5, baseline = (3*5-1)/2 = (15-1)/2=7, which matches optimal (no extra). For n=13, baseline = (39-1)/2=19, optimal is 23, which is +4. So extra moves increase.

Observation: extra moves seem to be (n-5)/4 *2? Let's compute difference moves - baseline:

n=5: diff=0
n=9: diff=2
n=13: diff=4
n=17: diff=6
So diff = (n-5)/2? Wait n=9 diff=2 = (9-5)/2=2 correct. n=13 diff=4 = (13-5)/2=4 correct. n=17 diff=6 = (17-5)/2=6 correct. So for n ≡ 1 mod 4, diff = (n-5)/2? Let's test n=5: (5-5)/2=0 correct. So moves = baseline + (n-5)/2 = (3n-1)/2 + (n-5)/2 = (4n -6)/2 = 2n - 3. Indeed matches formula.

So moves = baseline + (n-3)/2 = (3n-1)/2 + (n-3)/2 = (4n -4)/2 = 2n - 2. Matches formula.

Thus we have derived formula.

Now we need to prove minimality: lower bound.

We can argue as follows:

Let H be set of heads (even positions), T tails (odd positions). Let h = |H| = (n-1)/2, t = |T| = (n+1)/2.

In any walk, each time we move to a head, we flip it. Since we need final heads-up, each head must be flipped an even number of times. So total number of times we land on heads must be even for each head. Let x_i be number of visits to head i. Since each visit corresponds to a flip, parity condition: x_i even.

Similarly for each tail, visits must be odd.

Let total number of moves M = total visits = sum_i x_i.

Now consider total moves modulo 2: sum_i x_i = M. Sum over all vertices of parity constraints: sum_i x_i ≡ t (mod 2) because each tail contributes 1 (odd) and each head contributes 0 (even). So M ≡ t (mod 2). Since t = (n+1)/2. So M parity equals (n+1)/2 mod 2.

If n ≡ 1 mod 4, then t = (4k+2)/2 = 2k+1, odd. So M must be odd. If n ≡ 3 mod 4, t = (4k+4)/2 = 2k+2, even, so M even.

Now consider movement constraints: each move (except first) moves to an adjacent coin. So number of moves M equals 1 + number of edge traversals (since first flip doesn't involve moving). Edge traversals = M-1.

Each edge traversal moves from one vertex to adjacent. So each edge traversal contributes 1 to the visit count of the destination vertex. So total visits to heads plus tails equals M = 1 + total edge traversals.

Now we need lower bound on M based on needing to visit each tail at least once, and each head at least twice? Let's reason.

Observation: Since the coins alternate, to go from one tail to another tail you must pass through a head. Therefore any walk that visits all tails must also visit each intervening head at least once. Since there are h heads, each head is between two tails (except maybe edges). For interior heads (i.e., not at ends), they lie between two tails. To visit both neighboring tails, you must pass through the head at least once. However you could possibly start or end at a head, reducing one pass. But parity constraints require each head visited an even number of times, so at least 0 or 2 times. Since you need to pass through each interior head at least once (to get between tails), you must actually pass through it at least twice (once each direction) to make even. Edge cases at ends: first coin is tail, second is head. To reach the tail at position 1, you may start there (no need to pass through head). Similarly at far right end tail at position n, you may end there (no need to pass through preceding head). So we can potentially have the two end heads visited only once? Wait the heads at positions 2 and n-1 are adjacent to ends. Let's examine.

If we start at leftmost tail (position 1), we flip it (visit once). Then to reach next tail at position 3, we must go through head at position 2. Since head must be visited even times, we need to visit it at least twice (once forward, once backward) unless we end at head after final? But we need final state all heads up, so if we end at head after odd number of visits, its parity would be odd, not allowed. So we must ensure each head visited even times. So we need to visit head 2 at least twice.

Similarly for interior heads.

Thus each head must be visited at least twice, except possibly we could start at a head (giving first visit) and later visit it again to make even, still at least twice. So minimal visits to each head is 2.

Thus total minimum visits contributed by heads = 2h.

For tails: each tail must be visited odd times, minimal is 1. However we might need extra visits to tails due to back-and-forth needed to adjust heads parity. Indeed each time we do a back-and-forth on edge between a head and a tail, we increase visits to both by 1 (since we add arrival to each). This adds 1 extra visit to a tail (making it 2 visits total, which is even, not allowed). So we need to ensure tails stay odd; thus any extra visits to a tail must be in pairs (adding 2). So minimal extra visits to tails is 0 or 2 etc.

Thus lower bound: M >= 1 (first flip) + (minimum number of edge traversals needed to visit all tails and heads with required counts). But we can compute minimal total visits as: sum of minimal visits per vertex, but need to account for connectivity: the walk must be contiguous.

If each head visited exactly twice, each tail visited exactly once, total visits = h*2 + t*1 = 2h + t = 2*(n-1)/2 + (n+1)/2 = (n-1) + (n+1)/2 = (3n -1)/2 = baseline we computed earlier. However parity of total M must match t parity (odd/even). For n ≡ 1 mod 4, t odd, baseline (3n-1)/2 =? Let's compute parity: n=4k+1 => 3n-1 = 12k+3-1 = 12k+2, half = 6k+1, which is odd (since 6k even +1). So baseline is odd, matches required parity (odd). For n ≡ 3 mod 4, n=4k+3 => 3n-1 = 12k+9-1 = 12k+8, half = 6k+4, even, matches required parity (even). So baseline parity matches requirement. So baseline is feasible parity-wise.

Thus lower bound is at least baseline. However we also need to consider that the walk must start somewhere and end somewhere, which may affect counts for end vertices (they have one extra visit relative to arrivals). The minimal visits per vertex may not be simultaneously realizable due to start/end constraints.

Indeed for n ≡ 1 mod 4, we found optimal moves = baseline + (n-5)/2 = baseline + (t-2)/? Actually extra = (n-5)/2 = (t-3). For n=5, extra 0, feasible. For larger n, extra >0, meaning baseline not achievable; we need extra visits to some vertices (tails) to accommodate start/end parity.

This may force extra visits beyond baseline.

[CODE] (turn 1):
from collections import deque

def min_moves(n):
    # initial configuration: odd positions (1-indexed) are 1 (tails), even 0 (heads)
    init = 0
    for i in range(n):
        if (i+1)%2==1:  # odd index
            init |= (1<<i)
    # BFS over (config, pos) where pos is index of last flipped coin (0..n-1)
    # start from any possible first flip: we can choose any i, flip it.
    start_states = []
    for i in range(n):
        config = init ^ (1<<i)  # flip i
        start_states.append((config, i))
    # BFS
    INF = 10**9
    dist = {}
    q = deque()
    for state in start_states:
        dist[state] = 1  # one move performed
        q.append(state)
    target_config = 0
    while q:
        config, pos = q.popleft()
        d = dist[(config,pos)]
        if config == target_config:
            return d
        # possible next flips: adjacent positions
        for nxt in [pos-1, pos+1]:
            if 0 <= nxt < n:
                new_config = config ^ (1<<nxt)
                new_state = (new_config, nxt)
                if new_state not in dist:
                    dist[new_state] = d+1
                    q.append(new_state)
    return None

for n in range(1,13,2):
    print(n, min_moves(n))

def min_moves_bfs(n, limit=None):
    init = 0
    for i in range(n):
        if (i+1)%2==1:
            init |= (1<<i)
    target = 0
    from collections import deque
    q = deque()
    dist = {}
    for i in range(n):
        config = init ^ (1<<i)
        state = (config, i)
        dist[state] = 1
        q.append(state)
    while q:
        config, pos = q.popleft()
        d = dist[(config,pos)]
        if config == target:
            return d
        for nxt in (pos-1, pos+1):
            if 0 <= nxt < n:
                new_config = config ^ (1<<nxt)
                ns = (new_config, nxt)
                if ns not in dist:
                    dist[ns] = d+1
                    q.append(ns)
        if limit and len(dist) > limit:
            return None
    return None

    target = 0

def get_path(n):
    init = 0
    for i in range(n):
        if (i+1)%2==1:
            init |= (1<<i)
    target = 0
    from collections import deque
    q = deque()
    parent = {}
    dist = {}
    for i in range(n):
        config = init ^ (1<<i)
        state = (config, i)
        dist[state] = 1
        parent[state] = None
        q.append(state)
    while q:
        config, pos = q.popleft()
        d = dist[(config,pos)]
        if config == target:
            # reconstruct path
            path = []
            cur = (config,pos)
            while cur is not None:
                path.append(cur[1])  # position index
                cur = parent[cur]
            path.reverse()
            return path
        for nxt in (pos-1, pos+1):
            if 0 <= nxt < n:
                new_config = config ^ (1<<nxt)
                ns = (new_config, nxt)
                if ns not in dist:
                    dist[ns] = d+1
                    parent[ns] = (config,pos)
                    q.append(ns)
    return None

def apply_path(n, path):
    config = 0
    for i in range(n):
        if (i+1)%2==1:
            config |= (1<<i)
    for p in path:
        config ^= (1<<p)
    return config

[CODE] (turn 10):
def construct_path(n):
    # assume n odd, n%4==1
    path = [2,1]  # start at position 2 (even), then 1
    # we are currently at position 1
    # We'll iterate over odd positions from 3 to n step 2
    for odd in range(3, n+1, 2):
        even = odd+1
        # move to even (right)
        path.append(even)
        # move to odd (right)
        path.append(odd)
        # move back to even (left)
        path.append(even)
        # move to odd (right) again
        path.append(odd)
    # after loop, we are at last odd n (since odd = n)
    # need to end at n-1 (even) to have even visited twice? Actually we visited n-1 (even) twice in loop when odd=n-2? Wait n is odd, n-1 even.
    # In loop, when odd = n-2? No odd goes up to n, so last iteration odd=n, even=n+1 (out of range). So need to stop before n.

def construct_path_mod1(n):
    # n odd, n%4==1
    path = [2,1,2]  # start at 2, then 1, back to 2
    pos = 2
    # while we haven't reached n
    while pos < n:
        # pos is even
        odd = pos + 1
        even = odd + 1
        # steps:
        path.append(odd)   # to odd
        path.append(even)  # to even
        next_odd = odd + 2
        path.append(next_odd)  # to next odd
        path.append(even)   # back to even
        path.append(next_odd) # to next odd again
        # move to next even (prepare for next iteration)
        next_even = next_odd + 1
        if next_even <= n:
            path.append(next_even)
        pos = next_even
    return path

for n in [9,13,17]:
    p = construct_path_mod1(n)
    print(n, len(p), p[-5:])

    while pos < n:
        odd = pos + 1
        even = odd + 1
        path.append(odd)
        path.append(even)
        next_odd = odd + 2
        path.append(next_odd)
        path.append(even)
        path.append(next_odd)
        next_even = next_odd + 1
        if next_even <= n:
            path.append(next_even)
        pos = next_even
        steps += 1

def visit_counts(path, n):
    counts = [0]*n
    for p in path:
        counts[p-1] += 1
    return counts

[REASONING] (turn 16):
Counts for positions 1..13: [1,2,1,2,2,1,1,2,2,1,1,2,2]. Let's map parity: odds (1,3,5,7,9,11,13) have counts: 1,1,2,1,2,1,2. Some odds have even counts (2) which is not allowed. So our algorithm fails to meet parity constraints for larger n.

Thus we need a more sophisticated construction.

But we already have proven minimal moves formula using parity and lower bound arguments, and we have constructive examples for small n via BFS. For large n, we can claim existence of walk achieving bound by induction: we can extend optimal walk for n to n+4 by adding a pattern of 8 moves.

Observation: For n ≡ 1 mod 4, minimal moves = 2n - 3. For n+4 (also ≡ 1 mod 4), we can take optimal walk for n and extend by adding 8 moves to cover the new four positions at the right end.

Thus we can construct by induction: base case n=1 (1 move). For n+4, we take optimal walk for n, then after reaching position n (odd), we need to flip new coins at positions n+1 (even), n+2 (odd), n+3 (even), n+4 (odd). We can append a pattern of 8 moves that flips these correctly while preserving parity of earlier coins.

Let's attempt to design such extension pattern.

Suppose we have a walk that ends at position n (odd) with all coins up to n heads. Now we need to handle new segment of length 4: positions n+1 (even), n+2 (odd), n+3 (even), n+4 (odd). We can start from n (odd) and perform a sequence that results in final configuration all heads up to n+4, ending at n+3 (even) maybe, with total additional moves 8.

Let's try to find pattern of length 8 that starts at odd n, ends at even n+3, and flips the four new coins appropriately while not disturbing earlier coins (since we will not revisit them). Since we start at n (odd) which is already heads, flipping it would turn to tails, which is not allowed. So we need to start by moving to n+1 (even) (flip it), then n+2 (odd) (flip), then n+1 (even) again (flip back), then n+2 (odd) again (flip back?), need to ensure final parity.

Let's brute force search for a short pattern that flips the four new coins correctly while starting at n (odd) and ending at n+3 (even) without revisiting earlier positions (i.e., staying within new segment). Since we cannot affect earlier coins, we must not go left of n after start.

Let's brute force search for minimal sequence length for a segment of length 4 with start at leftmost odd (position 0) and need to end with all heads (0) for the segment (including start coin maybe unchanged). Actually start coin (position 0) is already heads, we must not flip it odd number of times. So we need to start at position 0 (odd) but not flip it? Wait the move at start flips the coin we are at. Since we are at position n (odd) after previous walk, the next move will flip a coin adjacent to it, i.e., position n+1. So we will not flip n again unless we later return. So we can keep n unchanged.

Thus we need to find a walk starting at position n+1 (first move flips n+1) but we are currently at n (odd). So the first move is to n+1 (flip it). So the start of new segment is at n+1 (even) after flipping it. Then we continue within new segment.

Goal: after completing the extension, all new coins (n+1..n+4) should be heads, and we end at some position (maybe n+3 even) to allow further extensions.

Let's brute force search for minimal sequence of moves within positions {n, n+1, n+2, n+3, n+4} where we start at n (odd) but first move flips n+1, and we cannot flip n again (or we could but need even total flips for n). Since n already heads, we need to flip it even number of times (0 or 2...). To keep minimal, we avoid flipping n again.

Thus we restrict to positions n+1..n+4 after first move.

Let's brute force search for minimal sequence of moves (positions) that starts at n+1 (after first move) and ends anywhere (maybe n+3) such that final configuration of positions n+1..n+4 is all heads (0), given initial pattern for those four positions: n+1 even (tails), n+2 odd (heads), n+3 even (tails), n+4 odd (heads). Wait initial pattern: original alternating pattern continues: position n+1 is even => heads? Wait original pattern: odd positions tails, even heads. Since n is odd, n+1 is even => heads initially. Let's verify: For n odd, pattern: odd T, even H. So n odd => T, n+1 even => H, n+2 odd => T, n+3 even => H, n+4 odd => T. So initial states for new segment: H, T, H, T (positions n+1..n+4). Goal: all heads (H). So we need to flip positions n+2 and n+4 (odd) an odd number of times, and positions n+1 and n+3 (even) an even number of times.

Thus we need to design walk starting at n+1 (even) after flipping it (so its state toggles to T). So we need to flip n+1 again later to make it H (even total). Similarly for n+3.

Thus we need to flip each even twice, each odd once (or three times). Minimal total flips for this 4-coin segment, given we start at n+1 after first flip (so we have already flipped n+1 once), is maybe 8 moves total (including the first move from n to n+1). Let's brute force search for minimal additional moves after the first move (i.e., after we have flipped n+1 once) to achieve final all heads, ending at some position (maybe n+3). [CODE] (turn 1):
from collections import deque

def min_moves(n):
    # initial state: alternating T (1) H (0) starting with T at position 0 (index 0)
    init = tuple(1 if i%2==0 else 0 for i in range(n))
    target = tuple(0 for _ in range(n))
    # BFS over (state, position) where position is index of last flipped coin
    # first move can be any coin.
    # Represent state as tuple of bits.
    visited = {}
    q = deque()
    # initial moves: flip any coin i
    for i in range(n):
        s = list(init)
        s[i] ^= 1
        state = tuple(s)
        key = (state, i)
        visited[key] = 1
        q.append(key)
    steps = 1
    while q:
        for _ in range(len(q)):
            state, pos = q.popleft()
            if state == target:
                return steps
            # next moves: flip adjacent coin
            for nb in [pos-1, pos+1]:
                if 0 <= nb < n:
                    s = list(state)
                    s[nb] ^= 1
                    new_state = tuple(s)
                    key = (new_state, nb)
                    if key not in visited:
                        visited[key] = steps+1
                        q.append(key)
        steps += 1
    return None

for n in range(1,11,2):
    print(n, min_moves(n))

from collections import deque

def min_moves(n):
    # initial state: alternating T (1) H (0) starting with T at position 0 (index 0)
    init = tuple(1 if i%2==0 else 0 for i in range(n))
    target = tuple(0 for _ in range(n))
    # BFS over (state, position) where position is index of last flipped coin
    # first move can be any coin.
    # Represent state as tuple of bits.
    visited = {}
    q = deque()
    # initial moves: flip any coin i
    for i in range(n):
        s = list(init)
        s[i] ^= 1
        state = tuple(s)
        key = (state, i)
        visited[key] = 1
        q.append(key)
    steps = 1
    while q:
        for _ in range(len(q)):
            state, pos = q.popleft()
            if state == target:
                return steps
            # next moves: flip adjacent coin
            for nb in [pos-1, pos+1]:
                if 0 <= nb < n:
                    s = list(state)
                    s[nb] ^= 1
                    new_state = tuple(s)
                    key = (new_state, nb)
                    if key not in visited:
                        visited[key] = steps+1
                        q.append(key)
        steps += 1
    return None

[CODE] (turn 2):
def min_moves_bfs(n):
    init = tuple(1 if i%2==0 else 0 for i in range(n))
    target = tuple(0 for _ in range(n))
    from collections import deque
    visited = set()
    q = deque()
    # first move any coin
    for i in range(n):
        s = list(init)
        s[i] ^= 1
        state = tuple(s)
        q.append((state, i, 1))
        visited.add((state,i))
    while q:
        state,pos,moves = q.popleft()
        if state == target:
            return moves
        for nb in (pos-1, pos+1):
            if 0 <= nb < n:
                s = list(state)
                s[nb] ^= 1
                new_state = tuple(s)
                key = (new_state, nb)
                if key not in visited:
                    visited.add(key)
                    q.append((new_state, nb, moves+1))
    return None

for n in range(1,15,2):
    print(n, min_moves_bfs(n))

def min_moves_bfs(n):
    init = tuple(1 if i%2==0 else 0 for i in range(n))
    target = tuple(0 for _ in range(n))
    from collections import deque
    visited = set()
    q = deque()
    # first move any coin
    for i in range(n):
        s = list(init)
        s[i] ^= 1
        state = tuple(s)
        q.append((state, i, 1))
        visited.add((state,i))
    while q:
        state,pos,moves = q.popleft()
        if state == target:
            return moves
        for nb in (pos-1, pos+1):
            if 0 <= nb < n:
                s = list(state)
                s[nb] ^= 1
                new_state = tuple(s)
                key = (new_state, nb)
                if key not in visited:
                    visited.add(key)
                    q.append((new_state, nb, moves+1))
    return None

for n in range(1,15,2):
    print(n, min_moves_bfs(n))

import itertools

def min_moves_ilp(n):
    # initial parity p_i: 1 for odd index (0-based? We'll use 0-index)
    p = [1 if i%2==0 else 0 for i in range(n)]  # index 0 corresponds to coin 1 (tails)
    best = None
    best_detail = None
    # enumerate start vertex s (0..n-1)
    for s in range(n):
        # compute r_i = p_i + (1 if i==s else 0) mod 2
        r = [(p[i] + (1 if i==s else 0)) % 2 for i in range(n)]
        # enumerate end vertex t (could be same as s)
        for t in range(n):
            # delta_i = out - in
            delta = [0]*n
            if s != t:
                delta[s] = 1
                delta[t] = -1

            # enumerate x_i, y_i for edges i=0..n-2 (edge between i and i+1)
            # limit to 0..2 for now

[CODE] (turn 4):
import itertools

def min_moves_ilp(n):
    # initial parity p_i: 1 for odd index (0-based? We'll use 0-index)
    p = [1 if i%2==0 else 0 for i in range(n)]  # index 0 corresponds to coin 1 (tails)
    best = None
    best_detail = None
    # enumerate start vertex s (0..n-1)
    for s in range(n):
        # compute r_i = p_i + (1 if i==s else 0) mod 2
        r = [(p[i] + (1 if i==s else 0)) % 2 for i in range(n)]
        # enumerate end vertex t (could be same as s)
        for t in range(n):
            # delta_i = out - in
            delta = [0]*n
            if s != t:
                delta[s] = 1
                delta[t] = -1
            # else delta all zero
            # enumerate x_i, y_i for edges i=0..n-2 (edge between i and i+1)
            # limit to 0..2 for now
            edges = n-1
            # brute force all combos of x_i,y_i in {0,1,2}
            # This is 3^(2*edges

from collections import deque

def min_moves(n):
    # n odd, initial pattern T H T H ... starting with T at position 0 (0-index)
    # Represent state as (bits, pos) where bits is tuple of 0/1 (0=T,1=H)
    # pos is index of last flipped coin (or None for start)
    start_bits = tuple(0 if i%2==0 else 1 for i in range(n))
    # BFS
    start = (start_bits, None)  # before first move, no position
    q = deque()
    q.append((start,0))
    visited = set()
    visited.add(start)
    target_bits = tuple([1]*n)
    while q:
        (bits,pos),dist = q.popleft()
        if bits == target_bits:
            return dist
        # generate moves
        if pos is None:
            # first move: any coin
            for i in range(n):
                newbits = list(bits)
                newbits[i] ^= 1
                newstate = (tuple(newbits), i)
                if newstate not in visited:
                    visited.add(newstate)
                    q.append((newstate, dist+1))
        else:
            for i in [pos-1, pos+1]:
                if 0 <= i < n:
                    newbits = list(bits)
                    newbits[i] ^= 1
                    newstate = (tuple(newbits), i)
                    if newstate not in visited:
                        visited.add(newstate)
                        q.append((newstate, dist+1))
    return None

for n in range(1,13,2):
    print(n, min_moves(n))

from collections import deque

def min_moves(n):
    start_bits = tuple(0 if i%2==0 else 1 for i in range(n))
    target_bits = tuple([1]*n)
    start = (start_bits, None)
    q = deque([(start,0)])
    visited = {start}
    while q:
        (bits,pos),dist = q.popleft()
        if bits == target_bits:
            return dist
        if pos is None:
            for i in range(n):
                newbits = list(bits)
                newbits[i] ^= 1
                newstate = (tuple(newbits), i)
                if newstate not in visited:
                    visited.add(newstate)
                    q.append((newstate, dist+1))
        else:
            for i in (pos-1, pos+1):
                if 0 <= i < n:
                    newbits = list(bits)
                    newbits[i] ^= 1
                    newstate = (tuple(newbits), i)
                    if newstate not in visited:
                        visited.add(newstate)
                        q.append((newstate, dist+1))
    return None

def min_moves_ilp(n):
    # n odd
    # variables order: m[0..n-2], t[0..n-1], b[0..n-1]
    num_m = n-1
    num_t = n
    num_b = n
    total_vars = num_m + num_t + num_b
    # objective: minimize sum of m_i
    c = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=float)
    c[:num_m] = 1.0  # minimize sum m_i
    
    # bounds: m_i >=0 integer, t_i >=0 integer, b_i binary 0/1
    bounds = []
    for i in range(num_m):
        bounds.append((0, None))
    for i in range(num_t):
        bounds.append((0, None))
    for i in range(num_b):
        bounds.append((0, 1))
    
    # integrality: all variables integer, but we can set integrality mask
    integrality = np.ones(total_vars, dtype=int)  # all integer
    
    # constraints: for each vertex i, degree equation
    A_eq = []
    b_eq = []
    for i in range(n):
        row = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=float)
        # degree = (i>0? m_{i-1}:0) + (i<n-1? m_i:0)
        if i > 0:
            row[i-1] = 1  # m_{i-1}
        if i < n-1:
            row[i] = 1    # m_i
        # t_i coefficient -4
        row[num_m + i] = -4
        # b_i coefficient +1 (since subtract b_i)
        row[num_m + num_t + i] = 1  # because equation: degree = 4*t_i + (r_i - b_i) => degree -4*t_i - r_i + b_i = 0 => degree -4*t_i + b_i = r_i
        # We'll move r_i to RHS
        A_eq.append(row)
        # RHS = r_i
        r_i = 2 if (i%2==0) else 0  # careful: i index 0 corresponds to vertex 1 (odd). Vertex 1 is odd index (1) => p_i=1 => r_i=2. So i even => vertex odd => r_i=2. i odd => vertex even => r_i=0.
        b_eq.append(r_i)
    # sum b_i <= 2
    A_ub = np.zeros((1, total_vars), dtype=float)
    A_ub[0, num_m+num_t:num_m+num_t+num_b] = 1
    b_ub = np.array([2.0])
    
    # Use milp
    res = opt.milp(c=c, A_eq=np.array(A_eq), b_eq=np.array(b_eq), A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub,
                   bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'presolve': True, 'disp': False})
    if not res.success:
        print("MILP failed", res.message)
        return None
    m_vals = res.x[:num_m]
    total_m = int(round(sum(m_vals)))
    moves = total_m + 1
    return moves

for n in range(3,21,2):
    print(n, min_moves_ilp(n))
